# Unit 19 linguistic and owned-edition mapping packet

This packet prepares review. It records an unsigned structural mapping proposal. It does not record a qualified review, an approved edition alignment, or an install decision.

The owned-edition mapping worksheet separates observed edition facts, the proposed lesson and printed page range, unresolved edition questions, and matters that still require qualified linguistic review. ISBN, owned-copy verification, variance, reviewer identity, qualification, date, and approval stay blank. The disposition remains PENDING_CURRICULUM_REVIEWER.

Unit 19 remains in development. Its readiness is content-complete and review-pending. The Lesson 19 relationship is a proposed, unsigned mapping. It is not an approved edition alignment.

body=отговаривать is the imperfective partner of perfective отговорить: both concern dissuading. For a question asking for the perfective partner of отговаривать, select отговорить. договориться is a different verb meaning to agree.

### STEP `u19-st-teach-conditional-1`

- Objectives: obj-u19-conditional
- Sessions: u19-s-conditional
- Source: none

- Current review status: NO_SEPARATE_REVIEW_FIELD
- Content hash: 0d59c2652d4943ee4d45a5f9d7631104749fa7aae336802bd1f09a81462eadcd

kind=TEACH
title=The past form with a conditional particle
body=я спал бы is used by a male speaker; я спала́ бы is used by a female speaker. These labels describe the speaker marked by the past-form agreement; they are not translations of the whole expressions. Both use the first-person pronoun, a past-form verb and the conditional particle. In this construction the past form need not report an actual past event: it can express a hypothetical situation, such as I would sleep. Context supplies the time reference.
dominantAction=Continue
references=ref-u1620-attribution

- Content hash: e43b4595c4e2d46d4800e91045dfd4a7767034456a8fb6096d3219fa62530894

kind=TEACH
title=Read agreement rather than an English sentence label
body=Compare the two retained forms я спал бы and я спала́ бы. The feminine past form has a final vowel that the masculine form lacks. For the first choose a male speaker; for the second choose a female speaker. I would like to sleep is a sentence meaning, not a label identifying the gender marked by the shown past form. Speaker agreement and sentence meaning are different questions.

### STEP `u19-st-teach-conditional-3`

- Objectives: obj-u19-conditional
- Sessions: u19-s-conditional
- Source: none
- Rights: none
- Classification: none
- Current review status: NO_SEPARATE_REVIEW_FIELD
- Content hash: 296dd4d207ea7d76fd570dd5585cac1f857998f089b09fdd75170820ed80fed6

kind=TEACH
title=A wish and a different position for the particle
body=я бы поспа́л — I would like to sleep. я бы поспала́ has feminine speaker agreement: the speaker is marked feminine. The first-person pronoun identifies the speaker, rather than another person. These retained wish expressions have the conditional particle before the verb, whereas the earlier examples put it after the verb. It cannot begin its clause. The wish reading comes from the construction and context, not from translating every conditional particle as would like. These choices do not demonstrate independently forming a conditional sentence or an aspect choice.
dominantAction=Continue

kind=REMEDIATE
title=Review the complete contrast
body=я спал бы is used by a male speaker; я спала́ бы is used by a female speaker. These labels describe the speaker marked by the past-form agreement; they are not translations of the whole expressions. Both use the first-person pronoun, a past-form verb and the conditional particle. In this construction the past form need not report an actual past event: it can express a hypothetical situation, such as I would sleep. Context supplies the time reference.

Compare the two retained forms я спал бы and я спала́ бы. The feminine past form has a final vowel that the masculine form lacks. For the first choose a male speaker; for the second choose a female speaker. I would like to sleep is a sentence meaning, not a label identifying the gender marked by the shown past form. Speaker agreement and sentence meaning are different questions.

я бы поспа́л — I would like to sleep. я бы поспала́ has feminine speaker agreement: the speaker is marked feminine. The first-person pronoun identifies the speaker, rather than another person. These retained wish expressions have the conditional particle before the verb, whereas the earlier examples put it after the verb. It cannot begin its clause. The wish reading comes from the construction and context, not from translating every conditional particle as would like. These choices do not demonstrate independently forming a conditional sentence or an aspect choice.

kind=TEACH
title=An obligation for a masculine speaker
body=я должен — I have to. This retained phrase expresses obligation for a masculine singular speaker. The short adjective agrees with the subject, and an infinitive can name the required action. The subject is nominative. The shown phrase does not name an action, so do not infer a particular task from it.
dominantAction=Continue

kind=TEACH
title=Feminine, neuter and plural agreement
body=должна — If the subject is feminine. должно — If the subject is neuter. должны — If the subject is plural. These retained English labels describe agreement; they are not literal translations of each Russian word. Select the feminine label for the feminine form and the plural label for the plural form. The short adjective changes with the subject, while the infinitive naming the action does not acquire a gender ending.
dominantAction=Continue

title=Separate an agreement label from a meaning
body=I have to is the meaning of the retained first-person phrase. If the subject is feminine and If the subject is plural are labels describing which form to use. A question asking for an agreement label differs from one asking what the whole phrase means. Obligation is one use of this adjective; an expected-event reading is taught separately. These selections do not demonstrate independently producing an obligation statement or using all tense forms.

kind=TEACH
title=An expected eclipse
body=Затме́ние должно́ наступи́ть в три часа́. — The eclipse should occur at three o'clock. Here should expresses an expected event, not a duty imposed on the eclipse. The short adjective agrees with the neuter subject and the infinitive names the event. The retained usage description is (with a verb) Used to express likelihood. Usually translated as "should".
dominantAction=Continue

kind=TEACH
title=An employee obligation
body=Слу́жащие должны́ во́время явля́ться на рабо́ту. — Employees must report to work on time. This states an obligation for plural employees. The English label an obligation describes a required action; an expected event describes a prediction such as the eclipse. Context determines which sense the shared adjective-plus-infinitive structure expresses. One independent check asks for the eclipse sentence meaning; the other classifies the employee obligation. These two examples do not demonstrate independent sentence production or every use of the adjective.

я бы поспала́
the speaker is marked feminine

поспала́ marks a feminine speaker. The wish to sleep is a different question.
The feminine past поспала́ marks the speaker as feminine in this conditional.
the speaker is marked masculine

the sentence is about another person is not what the first-person pronoun states.

### PROVENANCE `prov-ca-u19-instruction-conditional-rem-body`

- Objectives: obj-u19-conditional
- Sessions: u19-s-orient, u19-s-conditional
- Source: src-content-audit-research
- Rights: rights-content-audit-research
- Classification: none
- Current review status: IMPLEMENTED_PENDING_FINAL_AUDIT
- Content hash: 382c0cb3f03f28c5ea604ca3b6656c9f16a5bf77b90448e2bfeeb1f2496b41d8

recordType=label
sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:content-audit-research:2026-10-08
sourceRevision=content-audit-2026-10-08
sourceRecordId=ca-coverage:obj-u19-conditional:instruction:conditional-rem-body
originalSourceText=Retained Unit19 Wikipedia grammar, Wikibooks obligation and Wiktionary dolzhen revision84965088. Primary Cornell grammar le87_94_d.htm, gr02_c_1.htm and le95_102_c.htm, https://russian.cornell.edu/grammar/html/, consulted2026-10-11: conditional, agreement and obligation. Independent English synthesis; linguistic review pending.
я спал бы is used by a male speaker; я спала́ бы is used by a female speaker. These labels describe the speaker marked by the past-form agreement; they are not translations of the whole expressions. Both use the first-person pronoun, a past-form verb and the conditional particle. In this construction the past form need not report an actual past event: it can express a hypothetical situation, such as I would sleep. Context supplies the time reference.

Compare the two retained forms я спал бы and я спала́ бы. The feminine past form has a final vowel that the masculine form lacks. For the first choose a male speaker; for the second choose a female speaker. I would like to sleep is a sentence meaning, not a label identifying the gender marked by the shown past form. Speaker agreement and sentence meaning are different questions.

я бы поспа́л — I would like to sleep. я бы поспала́ has feminine speaker agreement: the speaker is marked feminine. The first-person pronoun identifies the speaker, rather than another person. These retained wish expressions have the conditional particle before the verb, whereas the earlier examples put it after the verb. It cannot begin its clause. The wish reading comes from the construction and context, not from translating every conditional particle as would like. These choices do not demonstrate independently forming a conditional sentence or an aspect choice.

In this construction the past form need not report an actual past event: it can express a hypothetical situation, such as I would sleep. Context supplies the time reference.

originalSourceText=Retained Unit19 Wikipedia grammar, Wikibooks obligation and Wiktionary dolzhen revision84965088. Primary Cornell grammar le87_94_d.htm, gr02_c_1.htm and le95_102_c.htm, https://russian.cornell.edu/grammar/html/, consulted2026-10-11: conditional, agreement and obligation. Independent English synthesis; linguistic review pending.
Compare the two retained forms я спал бы and я спала́ бы. The feminine past form has a final vowel that the masculine form lacks. For the first choose a male speaker; for the second choose a female speaker. I would like to sleep is a sentence meaning, not a label identifying the gender marked by the shown past form. Speaker agreement and sentence meaning are different questions.

Speaker agreement and sentence meaning are different questions.

originalSourceText=Retained Unit19 Wikipedia grammar, Wikibooks obligation and Wiktionary dolzhen revision84965088. Independent English synthesis; linguistic review pending.
я бы поспа́л — I would like to sleep. я бы поспала́ has feminine speaker agreement: the speaker is marked feminine. The first-person pronoun identifies the speaker, rather than another person. These retained wish expressions have the conditional particle before the verb, whereas the earlier examples put it after the verb. It cannot begin its clause. The wish reading comes from the construction and context, not from translating every conditional particle as would like. These choices do not demonstrate independently forming a conditional sentence or an aspect choice.
shippedText=я бы поспа́л — I would like to sleep. я бы поспала́ has feminine speaker agreement: the speaker is marked feminine. The first-person pronoun identifies the speaker, rather than another person. These retained wish expressions have the conditional particle before the verb, whereas the earlier examples put it after the verb. It cannot begin its clause. The wish reading comes from the construction and context, not from translating every conditional particle as would like. These choices do not demonstrate independently forming a conditional sentence or an aspect choice.

- Content hash: c5aef8d8c57e22921f3065752c428b5a6ba21489146231f6395270c5e5c84f84

recordType=label
sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:content-audit-research:2026-10-08
sourceRevision=content-audit-2026-10-08
sourceRecordId=ca-coverage:obj-u19-obligation:instruction:obligation-rem-body
originalSourceText=Retained Unit19 Wikipedia grammar, Wikibooks obligation and Wiktionary dolzhen revision84965088. Primary Cornell grammar le87_94_d.htm, gr02_c_1.htm and le95_102_c.htm, https://russian.cornell.edu/grammar/html/, consulted2026-10-11: conditional, agreement and obligation. Independent English synthesis; linguistic review pending.
я должен — I have to. This retained phrase expresses obligation for a masculine singular speaker. The short adjective agrees with the subject, and an infinitive can name the required action. The subject is nominative. The shown phrase does not name an action, so do not infer a particular task from it.

должна — If the subject is feminine. должно — If the subject is neuter. должны — If the subject is plural. These retained English labels describe agreement; they are not literal translations of each Russian word. Select the feminine label for the feminine form and the plural label for the plural form. The short adjective changes with the subject, while the infinitive naming the action does not acquire a gender ending.

I have to is the meaning of the retained first-person phrase. If the subject is feminine and If the subject is plural are labels describing which form to use. A question asking for an agreement label differs from one asking what the whole phrase means. Obligation is one use of this adjective; an expected-event reading is taught separately. These selections do not demonstrate independently producing an obligation statement or using all tense forms.
shippedText=я должен — I have to. This retained phrase expresses obligation for a masculine singular speaker. The short adjective agrees with the subject, and an infinitive can name the required action. The subject is nominative. The shown phrase does not name an action, so do not infer a particular task from it.

должна — If the subject is feminine. должно — If the subject is neuter. должны — If the subject is plural. These retained English labels describe agreement; they are not literal translations of each Russian word. Select the feminine label for the feminine form and the plural label for the plural form. The short adjective changes with the subject, while the infinitive naming the action does not acquire a gender ending.

I have to is the meaning of the retained first-person phrase. If the subject is feminine and If the subject is plural are labels describing which form to use. A question asking for an agreement label differs from one asking what the whole phrase means. Obligation is one use of this adjective; an expected-event reading is taught separately. These selections do not demonstrate independently producing an obligation statement or using all tense forms.

I have to is the meaning of the retained first-person phrase. If the subject is feminine and If the subject is plural are labels describing which form to use. A question asking for an agreement label differs from one asking what the whole phrase means. Obligation is one use of this adjective; an expected-event reading is taught separately. These selections do not demonstrate independently producing an obligation statement or using all tense forms.

- Content hash: d804f53377039dd0d0f3a9cac13269fa2cfdb0b92b34b8583f3fa72d85e3f331

recordType=label
sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:content-audit-research:2026-10-08
sourceRevision=content-audit-2026-10-08
sourceRecordId=ca-coverage:obj-u19-probability:instruction:probability-rem-body
originalSourceText=Retained Unit19 Wikipedia grammar, Wikibooks obligation and Wiktionary dolzhen revision84965088. Primary Cornell grammar le87_94_d.htm, gr02_c_1.htm and le95_102_c.htm, https://russian.cornell.edu/grammar/html/, consulted2026-10-11: conditional, agreement and obligation. Independent English synthesis; linguistic review pending.
Затме́ние должно́ наступи́ть в три часа́. — The eclipse should occur at three o'clock. Here should expresses an expected event, not a duty imposed on the eclipse. The short adjective agrees with the neuter singular subject; the following infinitive names the event. The retained source description is (with a verb) Used to express likelihood. Usually translated as "should".

Слу́жащие должны́ во́время явля́ться на рабо́ту. — Employees must report to work on time. This example states an obligation for plural employees rather than predicting an eclipse. Meaning comes from context as well as the shared adjective-plus-infinitive structure. In the likelihood selection choose the eclipse example. For its full English meaning choose the entire sentence, not the dictionary usage label (with a verb). Both independent checks currently repeat this same eclipse sentence; recognizing it twice does not demonstrate broader sense discrimination or independent sentence production.
shippedText=Затме́ние должно́ наступи́ть в три часа́. — The eclipse should occur at three o'clock. Here should expresses an expected event, not a duty imposed on the eclipse. The short adjective agrees with the neuter singular subject; the following infinitive names the event. The retained source description is (with a verb) Used to express likelihood. Usually translated as "should".

Слу́жащие должны́ во́время явля́ться на рабо́ту. — Employees must report to work on time. This example states an obligation for plural employees rather than predicting an eclipse. Meaning comes from context as well as the shared adjective-plus-infinitive structure. In the likelihood selection choose the eclipse example. For its full English meaning choose the entire sentence, not the dictionary usage label (with a verb). Both independent checks currently repeat this same eclipse sentence; recognizing it twice does not demonstrate broader sense discrimination or independent sentence production.

### PROVENANCE `prov-ca-u1920-prefix-rem-body`

- Objectives: obj-u19-prefix-caution
- Sessions: u19-s-orient, u19-s-prefix-caution
- Source: src-content-audit-research
- Rights: rights-content-audit-research
- Classification: none
- Current review status: IMPLEMENTED_PENDING_FINAL_AUDIT
- Content hash: a5781b9af92b1adb8a381ba951ac136a10ebca3e925714a9a863eb38006b3b9d

recordType=label
sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:content-audit-research:2026-10-08
sourceRevision=content-audit-2026-10-08
sourceRecordId=ca-coverage:obj-u19-prefix-caution:prefix-rem-body
originalSourceText=Retained Wikibooks Russian aspect paragraph explicitly pairs отговаривать with отговорить; Gramota https://gramota.ru/meta/otgovorit and /meta/otgovarivat corroborate the pair. Retained Wikipedia motion table and examples, and Wiktionary dolzhen revision84965088. Cornell Beginning Russian Grammar https://russian.cornell.edu/grammar/html/gr12_b_a.htm le57_62_a.htm (directionality), le57_62_b.htm (setting out), and subject index consulted2026-10-11. Independent English synthesis; linguistic review pending.
отговорить — to dissuade. договориться — to agree. These are different meanings, even though the words share material with a verb of speaking. A prefix is not always just an aspect marker: learn the whole verb and its meaning.

отговаривать is the imperfective partner of perfective отговорить: both concern dissuading. The retained source explicitly identifies this pair. сказать belongs to a separate speaking pair; it is not the imperfective partner of отговорить. For a question asking for the perfective partner of отговаривать, select отговорить. договориться is a different verb meaning to agree.

написать — to write. сделать — to do. Neither expresses dissuading or agreeing. When a question asks for a meaning, translate the displayed verb. When it asks for an aspect partner, preserve the lexical meaning while changing aspect. These selections do not demonstrate creating new prefixed verbs or using aspect in a sentence.
shippedText=отговорить — to dissuade. договориться — to agree. These are different meanings, even though the words share material with a verb of speaking. A prefix is not always just an aspect marker: learn the whole verb and its meaning.

отговаривать is the imperfective partner of perfective отговорить: both concern dissuading. The retained source explicitly identifies this pair. сказать belongs to a separate speaking pair; it is not the imperfective partner of отговорить. For a question asking for the perfective partner of отговаривать, select отговорить. договориться is a different verb meaning to agree.

написать — to write. сделать — to do. Neither expresses dissuading or agreeing. When a question asks for a meaning, translate the displayed verb. When it asks for an aspect partner, preserve the lexical meaning while changing aspect. These selections do not demonstrate creating new prefixed verbs or using aspect in a sentence.

The retained source explicitly identifies this pair. сказать belongs to a separate speaking pair; it is not the imperfective partner of отговорить. For a question asking for the perfective partner of отговаривать, select отговорить. договориться is a different verb meaning to agree.

recordType=label
sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:content-audit-research:2026-10-08
sourceRevision=content-audit-2026-10-08
sourceRecordId=ca-coverage:obj-u19-probability:probability-rem-body
originalSourceText=Retained Wikibooks Russian aspect paragraph explicitly pairs отговаривать with отговорить; Gramota https://gramota.ru/meta/otgovorit and /meta/otgovarivat corroborate the pair. Retained Wikipedia motion table and examples, and Wiktionary dolzhen revision84965088. Cornell Beginning Russian Grammar https://russian.cornell.edu/grammar/html/gr12_b_a.htm le57_62_a.htm (directionality), le57_62_b.htm (setting out), and subject index consulted2026-10-11. Independent English synthesis; linguistic review pending.
Затме́ние должно́ наступи́ть в три часа́. — The eclipse should occur at three o'clock. Here should expresses an expected event, not a duty imposed on the eclipse. The short adjective agrees with the neuter subject and the infinitive names the event. The retained usage description is (with a verb) Used to express likelihood. Usually translated as "should".

Слу́жащие должны́ во́время явля́ться на рабо́ту. — Employees must report to work on time. This states an obligation for plural employees. The English label an obligation describes a required action; an expected event describes a prediction such as the eclipse. Context determines which sense the shared adjective-plus-infinitive structure expresses. One independent check asks for the eclipse sentence meaning; the other classifies the employee obligation. These two examples do not demonstrate independent sentence production or every use of the adjective.
shippedText=Затме́ние должно́ наступи́ть в три часа́. — The eclipse should occur at three o'clock. Here should expresses an expected event, not a duty imposed on the eclipse. The short adjective agrees with the neuter subject and the infinitive names the event. The retained usage description is (with a verb) Used to express likelihood. Usually translated as "should".

Слу́жащие должны́ во́время явля́ться на рабо́ту. — Employees must report to work on time. This states an obligation for plural employees. The English label an obligation describes a required action; an expected event describes a prediction such as the eclipse. Context determines which sense the shared adjective-plus-infinitive structure expresses. One independent check asks for the eclipse sentence meaning; the other classifies the employee obligation. These two examples do not demonstrate independent sentence production or every use of the adjective.

Cornell Beginning Russian Grammar https://russian.cornell.edu/grammar/html/gr12_b_a.htm le57_62_a.htm (directionality), le57_62_b.htm (setting out), and subject index consulted2026-10-11. Independent English synthesis; linguistic review pending.
Затме́ние должно́ наступи́ть в три часа́. — The eclipse should occur at three o'clock. Here should expresses an expected event, not a duty imposed on the eclipse. The short adjective agrees with the neuter subject and the infinitive names the event. The retained usage description is (with a verb) Used to express likelihood. Usually translated as "should".

originalSourceText=Retained Wikibooks Russian aspect paragraph explicitly pairs отговаривать with отговорить; Gramota https://gramota.ru/meta/otgovorit and /meta/otgovarivat corroborate the pair. Retained Wikipedia motion table and examples, and Wiktionary dolzhen revision84965088. Cornell Beginning Russian Grammar https://russian.cornell.edu/grammar/html/gr12_b_a.htm le57_62_a.htm (directionality), le57_62_b.htm (setting out), and subject index consulted2026-10-11. Independent English synthesis; linguistic review pending.
Слу́жащие должны́ во́время явля́ться на рабо́ту. — Employees must report to work on time. This states an obligation for plural employees. The English label an obligation describes a required action; an expected event describes a prediction such as the eclipse. Context determines which sense the shared adjective-plus-infinitive structure expresses. One independent check asks for the eclipse sentence meaning; the other classifies the employee obligation. These two examples do not demonstrate independent sentence production or every use of the adjective.
shippedText=Слу́жащие должны́ во́время явля́ться на рабо́ту. — Employees must report to work on time. This states an obligation for plural employees. The English label an obligation describes a required action; an expected event describes a prediction such as the eclipse. Context determines which sense the shared adjective-plus-infinitive structure expresses. One independent check asks for the eclipse sentence meaning; the other classifies the employee obligation. These two examples do not demonstrate independent sentence production or every use of the adjective.

sourceRevision=1369617627
sourceRecordId=grammar:The [[conditional mood]] in Russian is formed by adding the particle бы after th
originalSourceText=The [[conditional mood]] in Russian is formed by adding the particle бы after the word which marks the supposed subject into a sentence formed like in the past tense. Thus, to say "I would (hypothetically) sleep" or "I would like to sleep", a male speaker would say я спал бы (or я бы поспа́л), while a female speaker would say я спала́ бы (or я бы поспала́).
shippedText=a male speaker
transformation=selection

originalSourceText=The [[conditional mood]] in Russian is formed by adding the particle бы after the word which marks the supposed subject into a sentence formed like in the past tense. Thus, to say "I would (hypothetically) sleep" or "I would like to sleep", a male speaker would say я спал бы (or я бы поспа́л), while a female speaker would say я спала́ бы (or я бы поспала́).

- Content hash: 852f7a16a246cd14956dfc2e0c7885489f3a0e60ee4f5a510c9b767669f50378

recordType=meaning
sourceUrl=https://en.wikibooks.org/wiki/Russian/Verbal_Aspect?oldid=4059032
sourceRevision=4059032
sourceRecordId=aspect:Many verbs come in pairs like '''делать/сделать''', with the perfective variant 
originalSourceText=Many verbs come in pairs like '''делать/сделать''', with the perfective variant simply being the imperfective with a prefix. For instance, '''написать''' is the perfective of '''писать''' ('to write'), '''посмотреть''' is the perfective of '''смотреть''' ('to watch'), and we've already seen the imperfective '''делать''' and the perfective '''сделать''' ('to do'). For such verbs, the aspectual pair will often be given in dictionaries as a prefix indicated in parentheses: '''(с)делать, (по)смотреть, (на)писать''', etc. Such pairs of aspects are easy to learn, as the perfective also conjugates the same as the imperfective, only with a prefix.

However, one has to be careful with prefixes. While the perfective can be formed by a prefix, other prefixes can be applied to an imperfective to make a whole new verbal pair. For instance, we can add the prefix '''от''' to the imperfective '''говорить''' to make the new perfective '''отговорить''', meaning 'to dissuade'. From this, we can derive a new imperfective aspect, '''отговаривать'''. Another aspectual pair we can derive from '''говорить''' is '''договариваться''' (impf.) and '''договориться''' (perf.), meaning "to agree". So we have three pairs derived using prefixes:
: '''говорить''' (impf.) and '''сказать''' (pf.)
: '''отговаривать''' (impf.) and '''отговорить''' (pf.)
: '''договариваться''' (impf.) and '''договориться''' (pf.)
In other words, the easiest way to remember aspectual pairs is to learn the imperfective and learn the 'perfective prefix'. The prefix itself is largely unpredictable (the most common is '''по-''', and others are '''про-''', '''на-''', '''за-''', etc), not all verbs form their perfective aspect with a prefix, and some prefixes can create whole new verbs.
shippedText=to dissuade
transformation=selection
licenseId=CC BY-SA 4.0
licenseUrl=https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/
attribution=Wikibooks Russian grammar contributors, CC BY-SA 4.0. Page history for each frozen oldid identifies the contributors.

sourceRecordId=aspect:Many verbs come in pairs like '''делать/сделать''', with the perfective variant 
originalSourceText=Many verbs come in pairs like '''делать/сделать''', with the perfective variant simply being the imperfective with a prefix. For instance, '''написать''' is the perfective of '''писать''' ('to write'), '''посмотреть''' is the perfective of '''смотреть''' ('to watch'), and we've already seen the imperfective '''делать''' and the perfective '''сделать''' ('to do'). For such verbs, the aspectual pair will often be given in dictionaries as a prefix indicated in parentheses: '''(с)делать, (по)смотреть, (на)писать''', etc. Such pairs of aspects are easy to learn, as the perfective also conjugates the same as the imperfective, only with a prefix.

However, one has to be careful with prefixes. While the perfective can be formed by a prefix, other prefixes can be applied to an imperfective to make a whole new verbal pair. For instance, we can add the prefix '''от''' to the imperfective '''говорить''' to make the new perfective '''отговорить''', meaning 'to dissuade'. From this, we can derive a new imperfective aspect, '''отговаривать'''. Another aspectual pair we can derive from '''говорить''' is '''договариваться''' (impf.) and '''договориться''' (perf.), meaning "to agree". So we have three pairs derived using prefixes:
: '''говорить''' (impf.) and '''сказать''' (pf.)
: '''отговаривать''' (impf.) and '''отговорить''' (pf.)
: '''договариваться''' (impf.) and '''договориться''' (pf.)
In other words, the easiest way to remember aspectual pairs is to learn the imperfective and learn the 'perfective prefix'. The prefix itself is largely unpredictable (the most common is '''по-''', and others are '''про-''', '''на-''', '''за-''', etc), not all verbs form their perfective aspect with a prefix, and some prefixes can create whole new verbs.
shippedText=отговорить
transformation=selection

sourceRecordId=grammar:The [[conditional mood]] in Russian is formed by adding the particle бы after th
originalSourceText=The [[conditional mood]] in Russian is formed by adding the particle бы after the word which marks the supposed subject into a sentence formed like in the past tense. Thus, to say "I would (hypothetically) sleep" or "I would like to sleep", a male speaker would say я спал бы (or я бы поспа́л), while a female speaker would say я спала́ бы (or я бы поспала́).
shippedText=a male speaker
transformation=selection

recordType=instruction
sourceUrl=https://en.wikibooks.org/wiki/Russian/Verbal_Aspect?oldid=4059032
sourceRevision=4059032
sourceRecordId=aspect:Many verbs come in pairs like '''делать/сделать''', with the perfective variant 
originalSourceText=Many verbs come in pairs like '''делать/сделать''', with the perfective variant simply being the imperfective with a prefix. For instance, '''написать''' is the perfective of '''писать''' ('to write'), '''посмотреть''' is the perfective of '''смотреть''' ('to watch'), and we've already seen the imperfective '''делать''' and the perfective '''сделать''' ('to do'). For such verbs, the aspectual pair will often be given in dictionaries as a prefix indicated in parentheses: '''(с)делать, (по)смотреть, (на)писать''', etc. Such pairs of aspects are easy to learn, as the perfective also conjugates the same as the imperfective, only with a prefix.

However, one has to be careful with prefixes. While the perfective can be formed by a prefix, other prefixes can be applied to an imperfective to make a whole new verbal pair. For instance, we can add the prefix '''от''' to the imperfective '''говорить''' to make the new perfective '''отговорить''', meaning 'to dissuade'. From this, we can derive a new imperfective aspect, '''отговаривать'''. Another aspectual pair we can derive from '''говорить''' is '''договариваться''' (impf.) and '''договориться''' (perf.), meaning "to agree". So we have three pairs derived using prefixes:
: '''говорить''' (impf.) and '''сказать''' (pf.)
: '''отговаривать''' (impf.) and '''отговорить''' (pf.)
: '''договариваться''' (impf.) and '''договориться''' (pf.)
In other words, the easiest way to remember aspectual pairs is to learn the imperfective and learn the 'perfective prefix'. The prefix itself is largely unpredictable (the most common is '''по-''', and others are '''про-''', '''на-''', '''за-''', etc), not all verbs form their perfective aspect with a prefix, and some prefixes can create whole new verbs.
shippedText=some prefixes can create whole new verbs.
transformation=selection
